# Rules

**Snapback 2 optimizes the edit-to-verified loop — the agent's building an app on it, and ours building it.**

Speed is the constraint. Everything below exists to keep the edit-to-verified loop short
and to stop this repository accreting apparatus faster than anyone can remove it. These
rules bind `snapback2/` and the LLPs numbered 1000 and up; Snapback 1 is baselined, not
exempt, and its numbers may only fall.

Each rule is **[check]** (enforced by `caps`) or **[review]** (enforced by a human).

## Budgets — every budget is a trade, not a limit

- **5 blocking checks, 60s total.** A sixth requires deleting one, in the same PR. **[check]**
- **59 documents in the working set** (`llp/current/`). Today's count. A 60th requires
  archiving one — which is just removing the link. Snapback 2 aims to leave this at 15;
  Snapback 1's links are archived at promotion. **[check]**
- **12 documents in the foundation** (`llp/foundation/`). **[check]**
- **This file: 700 words.** If it grows, something becomes a check or stops being a rule. **[check]**
- **1,500 lines per source file, baseline 204.** Generated files exempt — and generated
  files are built, not committed; so are benchmark run records under `benchmarks/runs/`,
  which are evidence, not source. The baseline is Snapback 1's oversize count on the day
  the rule landed; it may not grow, and `snapback2/` contributes zero to it. **[check]**

## Blocking checks

`caps` · `ref-check` · `snapback2/scripts/test.sh` · `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs --check` · `check-inventories`

## Loop shape

- **Every check reports all failures in one run.** Fail-fast is banned. **[check]**
- **Nothing blocks on anything slower than 60 seconds.** Slow verification runs on a
  schedule, attributed to the breaking commit, and files a bounded set of issues. **[review]**
- **Fix loops get 3 rounds.** Then stop: escalate to a human or descope. **[review]**

## Time budgets

Tracked every run of `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs` (appends `snapback2/metrics/loop.jsonl`).
A regression is a P0 with a name on it.

| | budget |
|---|---|
| Incremental rebuild after a one-line edit | 15s |
| Test what you just changed | 30s |
| Blocking gate suite | 60s |
| Full build, warm cache | 5 min |
| **`snapback2 check` on a program (the agent's product latency)** | **1s** |
| **`snapback2 doctor` boot-to-ready** | **0.5s** |

## Scope

- **`NOT-DOING.md` is binding.** Moving something onto the doing-list means writing why
  and taking something off. **[review]**
- **Delete; don't deprecate.** No compat shims or migration paths before promotion. **[review]**
- **Nothing is compiled, transpiled, or generated at runtime that could be built ahead of
  time.** **[review]**
- **A spec needs an implementer and a date, or it isn't written.** **[review]**
- **Only implemented specs bind.** A document with no running code behind it is a
  proposal, and a proposal cannot block a PR or justify a check. **[review]**

## Agents

- **Agents remove apparatus freely and add none.** An agent PR that adds a check, script,
  registry, config file, or design doc needs explicit human approval saying so. **[review]**
- **2026-09-06 session exception:** the user delegated the search implementation
  decisions; one `snapback2-core/build.rs` may generate pinned Unicode tables at
  build time, alongside the existing package build. No new gate, registry,
  runtime generator, or Unicode dependency is authorized by this exception. **[review]**
- **Snapback 2 LLPs are numbered 1000 and up**; `llp-create` must be told. **[review]**
- **Snapback 4 LLPs are numbered 3000 and up** (`snapback4/`, LLP 3000); it adopts these budgets
  and loop shape as its own and keeps its scope in `snapback4/NOT-DOING.md`. **[review]**

## Shared machines

- Warm, named lanes, one per stream of work. Never clone fresh; reuse the worktree.
- **Never `git stash`.** Use a temporary commit.
- Kill only PIDs you recorded at launch. Never by name or pattern.

## Summary

Snapback 4 is one of two parallel candidates for the successor to Snapback 2,
and the sibling of Snapback 3, not its successor. It builds
[LLP 2000.000](./2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md) as
written: the Snapback 2 core kept verbatim, two substrates flipped up front —
resources metered at runtime instead of priced statically, and a client that
replicates the viewer's declared partition instead of caching receipted
results — proved first on the hub fixture, with a deterministic simulator as
the primary harness. Snapback 3 ([LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md), led by
Codex / GPT-6 Astra) keeps the same two questions open and discovers their
answers in its first slice. Snapback 2 ([LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md))
continues as the incumbent and the control. All three are judged by one
pre-registered comparison on one clock.

This document records the author's decisions of 2026-09-12 that created the
track, restates the bet on one page so a reader can orient without the brief,
fixes the bakeoff protocol, draws the fence, and plans the first four weeks.
The design argument and its evidence stay in LLP 2000.000. Only running code
will bind (`rules/RULES.md`).

## 1. Decisions (author, 2026-09-12)

On 2026-09-12 Charlie Cheever asked whether Claude Fable 5.1 should lead a
Snapback 4 while Codex / GPT-6 Astra leads Snapback 3 and Snapback 2
continues, "and see which one turns out best." The lead answered yes with
conditions; the author accepted them ("Sounds good") and asked for this
document. The decisions below are therefore the lead's proposal as accepted
by the author. Where the author has not separately ruled, the text says so.

1. **Three tracks, one comparison.** Snapback 2 continues as the incumbent
   and the control. Snapback 3 builds LLP 2000; Snapback 4 builds LLP
   2000.000. This is a bakeoff of *bets* — discover the substrates in the
   slice versus decide them first — not of models. The result will say more
   about the bets than about the leads, and §3 is written so that it can.
2. **The name is Snapback 4.** Siblings, not a sequence: "4" does not mean
   "after 3." A separate number gives the track its own corpus root,
   directory, package identity and data directory with no rename when the
   bakeoff resolves, and files never move in LLP. If Snapback 4 loses, it is
   a number that never shipped. "Snapback 3b" was considered and rejected:
   it reads as a variant of 3, and this is a different bet.
3. **Snapback 4 builds LLP 2000.000 as written.** Its §14 open questions are
   the lead's to decide inside the track, recorded in §7 as they are
   decided. The author rules on the bar, the comparison and promotion (§3).
4. **One owner on the core.** The lead writes the engine, the store and the
   client contract. Codex lanes build the fixture generator, the reference
   applications, the device witnesses and the periphery. Blind reviews from
   two model families are reserved for the two disclosure-sensitive
   mechanisms: partition inversion of read rules (LLP 2000.000 §7.1) and
   the meter's cut behaviour on non-uniform scans (LLP 2000.000 §6). This
   departs from the standing rule that Codex codes and Claude orchestrates,
   deliberately, for the core only.
5. **The clock is four weeks** from the first commit under `snapback4/` to
   the first slice running on the hub fixture. At the clock the author kills
   the track, merges its substrates into a survivor, or continues it. There
   is no third month without a ruling.
6. **Shared and not shared.** Shared with Snapback 3: the LLP corpus, the
   Clique harness and scorers, the fixtures, the briefs, and the simulator
   once either track has one. Not shared: code, except the Snapback 2 pieces
   both are expected to copy (LLP 2000.000 §5.1).
7. **Snapback 2 keeps shipping and freezes new substrate work for the
   duration.** Fix-forward, the loop bar, acquisition and papercuts continue;
   no new certificate axes, client custody mechanisms or ceiling changes.
   This is accepted here as a condition of the bakeoff. Making it binding on
   concurrent Snapback 2 sessions needs one line in `rules/NOT-DOING.md`'s
   Moves ledger, which this document does not write.
8. **Reading load.** This root, updated in place, and one append-only
   journal, `snapback4/JOURNAL.md`. Sub-LLPs only for a decision that must
   outlive the journal. No panels beyond decision 4's two reviews.

## 2. The bet on one page

**Keep verbatim from Snapback 2** (LLP 2000.000 §5.1): the rule language
with its uniformity and hoist analysis, `decided_true` and the
structural-fact refusals; one data language, Quarry, with `--` comments,
`require … else CODE`, `shape`, `merge`, `walk`, `chunk`; the value model
and its conformance corpus; typed read and write states with no silent state
and the refusal envelope `{code, family, site, message, rewrite, guide}`; the
successor classifier (widening additive, checked narrowing, exact projection
ownership); `deliver`'s engine-computed audience, tombstones and
`bootstrap`; `schedule`, `job`, `chunk` and rehearsal; personas; `init`,
`dev`, `check`, `doctor`, `run`, `test`, `why`, `trace`, `guide`; H0
`deploy`, `rollback`, `secret`; channels; assets; the word-prefix index.

**Flip 1 — certify authorization, meter resources** (LLP 2000.000 §6). The
certificate keeps every read site's shape, uniformity, completeness class
and prediction eligibility, and stops pricing bytes and steps. A
deterministic meter charges examined rows, index entries, rule probes,
steps, heap, output and rows written as the program runs, on the server and
in the client interpreter alike. A query cut by the meter returns `capped`
with no continuation past the cut, the same observable outcome as a static
ceiling cut today; a mutation aborts with `E_BOUND` naming actual work; a
job is chunked. `check --cost` prints the structural cost class; `test`
prints measured meters on the hub fixture.

**Flip 2 — the viewer's partition is the unit of sync** (LLP 2000.000 §7).
`sync to <audience> last N by <index>` on a table makes the engine maintain,
per principal, the rows that principal may read, as a change log with a
horizon; a table without the word is online-only, and `check` says which
rule term prevented inversion. The client holds a replica of its partitions,
one watermark each, a durable outbox with client-minted ids, and the
interpreter; queries run locally and are `complete` inside the horizon.
Revocation is a scope tombstone; `until-revoked` and `delivered-history`
survive as the client's leave policy. Two client tiers — online-only
without a store, local-first with one — share one language and one server.

**The vocabulary is fixed before the mechanism** (LLP 2000.000 §8):

```ts
type Read<T> = { data: T; complete: boolean; fresh: boolean; since?: number; next?: Cursor }
            | { loading: true } | { denied: Refusal };
row.pending: boolean;
type Write = { state: "pending" } | { state: "sent"; seq: number } | { state: "failed"; why: Refusal };
type Link = "online" | "offline" | "signed-out";
```

A screen that needs a word not on that card is a defect in the mechanism,
not a gap in the card.

### 15.4 Supplied client sessions

Online and local constructors validate a supplied session before any transport,
device/storage work or timer. Require a non-array object with nonempty principal,
kind and token strings, a positive finite safe-integer expiresAt in milliseconds,
and valid optional scope/capExpiresAt fields. Legacy scope-less sessions remain
accepted. Expiry relative to a possibly skewed local clock is server authority;
shape validation must not reject a structurally valid past timestamp. Malformed
input throws E_SESSION with the offending field and a repair: pass the complete
Session returned by guest/login/signup or service minting, or use the existing
online token option for a bare bearer. Never include credentials in the error.
capExpiresAt without scope remains accepted legacy metadata; neither grants read authority.
Stored-session validation retains its existing auth restore path. Snapshot validated primitive fields so caller mutation cannot manufacture a NaN
timer later. Existing intentional full-session refresh and read/action expiry
semantics remain unchanged; shape validation grants no authority.

## 4. Staging clock

Wave-20 reporting amendment: LLP 3000.000.000.001 §10 changes the proof
advance return to `{now, fired, held}` and explains persisted boundary holds;
operator authority and clock execution semantics below are unchanged.

Only a store durably classified staging by deploy --staging may serve with
--clock-offset. Missing/unknown classification is production. This setting
is process-local, with the same signed-duration parser and monotone elapsed
clock as dev. No serve-time mechanism can reclassify an existing production store. Restart drops the offset unless explicitly
provided again; persisted timed-disclosure floors never retreat below their captured value.

Staging exposes the existing /dev/clock/status and /dev/clock/advance only
with any full service session: these are trusted operator credentials, as for
system jobs. Production refuses even with a service token, and an explicit
production serve offset refuses with E_CLOCK_DEV_ONLY before listener startup. Account, guest, read-only and unauthenticated
requests refuse. Recheck classification and session under the engine writer
before advancing. All fixture, reset, persona and dev-job doors remain closed.
Use the same advance implementation, schedule drain and ambiguous-reply law
as dev. CLI clock advance --release DIR mints its local service authority.
startServe accepts clockOffset and exposes now()/advance() through that
operator authority; it never changes store classification. Each helper control
mints a current staging-time operator session rather than caching a credential
across arbitrary time jumps. Ordinary user sessions retain normal expiry.

Hot release activation samples `shared.now()` under its engine writer, including
the persisted disclosure floor, and passes that one time through the core's
explicit `adopt_release_at`. A live preview probes the existing filesystem owner lock independently of
HTTP bind/address hints and acquires a stopped-store lock before taking its
SQLite snapshot, retaining that lock through inspection. A rollback preview
reuses its caller's existing ownership rather than probing its own lock as a
live host.
A live preview samples time through the same-owner,
store-bound existing private status protocol; no session is minted or clock
changed. A missing existing-store ownership sidecar refuses rather than assuming a
stopped host without exclusion; ordinary rollback preview can reestablish and
reuse its canonical ownership. A revision mismatch with the private database snapshot refuses as a
racing activation. Store contents and the clock are point-in-time samples, not
a freeze of later live writes. A stopped store's cold activation/preview uses
wall time, with the existing persisted disclosure floor applied by activation.
Positive and negative staging offsets are both covered by hot/preview tests.

# LLP 3000.000.000.001: Staging with real users

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Implemented
**Systems:** Snapback 4 deployment, password identity, development doors, proof kit
**Author:** Codex for Charlie Cheever (STAGE)
**Date:** 2026-10-18
**Revised:** 2026-10-22 (DX6 §11); 2026-10-21 (DX5 §10); 2026-10-19 (DX4 §§8–9); 2026-10-18
**Related:** [Release activation](./3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md), [Proof kit](./3000.011.003.000.000-packaged-proof-kit.rfc.md), [Store ownership](./3000.019-what-the-builder-still-wrote.rfc.md#34-p8-residency-you-can-see-and-a-cli-that-does-not-fight-the-dev-server)

## 1. Commission and boundary

Implementer: STAGE, 2026-10-18, commissioned by Charlie's wave-16 bakeoff
fixes #1/#4. Ten Muse probes used dev because password-role provisioning
had no single recipe. This amendment supplies that recipe using the existing
signup and service-seat authority. No system seed door is added to serve.
SEC owns self-promotion lints and security invariants; KIT4 owns unrelated
proof helpers, PROD2 owns production starter adaptation.

`deploy <dir> --staging` classifies a **new deployment store**, under its
exclusive owner, with durable metadata `deployment_environment=staging`.
An existing store with no classification is production, including legacy stores;
it cannot be relabelled. A staging store remains staging through deploy,
rollback, restart, copying and source edits. There is no promote/relabel flag:
production requires a separate fresh deployment without --staging and real
operator provisioning. --check never creates the classification. Repeating
--staging on an already staging store is allowed, including while serve runs;
it verifies the existing classification without changing it. Malicious edits by
the filesystem/database owner are outside the threat model, as for service tokens.
This is a deployment-lifetime boundary stronger than a mutable per-release flag:
production source or a seed plan cannot opt a production store into test setup.

## 2. Real staging accounts

`GET /auth/config` reports `development` independently of door availability,
`devDoors`, and `environment` (`development`, `staging`, or `production`).
Missing/unknown environment metadata is production. serve remains bearer-only
in both environments, with the same password verifiers, session lifetimes,
rules, rate limits, clock and data plane. No password hashing or grant bypass.

`startServe(dir, options)` additionally returns `operatorToken(service =
"operator", days = 1)` and `seedAccounts(options)`. Minting uses the existing
local CLI proof protocol, captures secret stdout in memory and never writes
tokens into proof logs. It confers only that service seat's existing authority.
Tokens may also be requested on production for first-admin setup.

`seedAccounts({roles, provision, args?})` creates N real password accounts per
role, then calls the declared mutation as service:operator; `args(account)`
maps the account to application arguments (default `{principal, role}`).
`provision` is a generated mutation handle or `{name}`, including qualified names such as `accounts.provision`. The returned records
contain email, password, principal, token and expiresAt, grouped by role.
`startServe(dir, {seedUsers: options})` performs the same setup before returning
and exposes those records as `sessions`. Before minting or creating accounts,
the helper requires environment=staging and development=false. Production,
legacy and dev hosts refuse. Inputs are validated before effects; signup is
sequential, bounded, respects identity throttling, and never retries an ambiguous
write. A fresh call creates a fresh random email/password set. Partial failure
does not claim rollback: some accounts/grants may exist; discard the disposable
staging deployment or inspect it before retrying. Application data is created
through ordinary authenticated mutations after provisioning.

Credential records are returned to the operator, never logged by the helper.
The tested guide writes them to an exclusively created 0600 file. They must
not enter source control or reports. There is no public provisioning HTTP
endpoint beyond the application's declared rule-checked mutation.

## 3. First admin

The tested guide uses password signup (which grants no role), then the
filesystem owner's `operatorToken()` to invoke an operator-only mutation.
The roster's insert rule independently requires service:operator. No "first
caller wins", empty-roster bypass, client-selected signup role, or permanent
operator credential in the frontend. The same recipe works on production
with explicit operator-selected email/password; bulk seedAccounts refuses there.
Privileged account tokens cannot mint service tokens. SEC owns lints for
self-bootstrapped global authority; the guide points to this ownership.

## 4. Development doors

For password-only schemas (`auth.password && !auth.guests`), dev disables
`/seed`, every `/dev/*` door (except the existing cryptographically protected
local token protocol), and persona headers by default. The pre-existing serve
GET /dev/latency inspection remains service-authenticated; it never admits an
unauthenticated caller and its reset remains refused. `dev
--unsafe-dev-doors` is explicit opt-in with a prominent warning; serve accepts
no such flag. Host/Origin loopback fences still apply. Door availability is
computed from the current adopted schema, so hot adoption to password-only
also closes them. Seed/reset, clock advance and dev job enqueue revalidate
against the engine schema under the writer immediately before privileged effects;
slow request bodies and requests queued behind adoption cannot retain authority.
Action admission/replay, session-route admission, persona cancellation and upload
owner state transitions likewise revalidate under their actual writer. Previously
admitted durable work retains the existing work-record contract; a refused
new request creates no claimable work.
Persona reads also revalidate against the engine used to evaluate the query,
sync page or asset grant, checking the current declared cast at that read’s
admission. In-flight reads retain their existing snapshot semantics: cast
publication does not lock out concurrent ordinary writes, and the engine
transaction rejects a replaced execution schema.
Change-poll presence entry revalidates under its engine. A detached old
schema is fenced by the engine transaction; a replacement password-only reader
cannot inherit a pre-adoption persona capability. A persona header must name an explicitly declared persona;
an empty declaration admits none. Persona-based schemas retain their ordinary
development workflow. /auth/config always reports development=true on dev,
even with closed doors, so probe tooling can refuse it.

`startDev` follows native persistent storage by default, consuming the old
--no-memory alias; --memory/--fresh explicitly request disposable storage.
The returned now() refuses with guidance if the clock door is closed. Native
readiness plus the per-launch proof marker still establish host identity when
/dev/status is unavailable. Existing fixture/clock tests explicitly opt into
unsafe dev doors when their password schema requires those tools. Generated
frontend proofs explicitly select --memory and --unsafe-dev-doors, preserving
disposable fixture isolation and repeatability under the persistent helper
default. Exact 0.4.30/0.4.31 proof templates are retained and safely adapted
by digest; custom scripts remain untouched. DAY5 owns other starter custody.

## 5. Evidence

Required: production relabelling refusal before writes; staging retention over
hot/cold release switches; real signup/login per role and operator-only grants;
self-grant and unauthenticated doors refused; default/explicit dev doors and
schema-adoption closure; persistent/default and disposable dev; private token
logs; the exact guide recipe executed from the packed tarball in Linux
qualification, including production bulk-seed refusal and first admin; three
copied wave-16 dev probes converted to serve. Full crate tests, affected client
tests, Darwin affected tests on the assigned Mac Studio, ref-check, docs budget
and root inventory/guide checks. Never use the full scripts/test.sh here;
qualify-linux --consumer-only complements the manually run crate suites.

## 6. Review record

authority through password-only adoption; a test initializer missed the new
field; qualified mutation handles refused; affected old fixtures needed explicit
unsafe-door opt-in. The writer now rechecks all privileged effects, with
deterministic slow-body/queued-request tests. The initializer, qualified-module
regression, diagnostic assertions and password witness fixture are corrected.
Verbatim review and provenance: [Codex r1](./reviews/3000.000.000.001-stage-r1.codex.txt).

# Frontend data and actionable notes

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Review
**Systems:** snapback4-client, snapback4-language, snapback4-cli
**Author:** Charlie Cheever (implementation by Codex)
**Date:** 2026-10-18
**Revised:** 2026-10-18
**Related:** LLP 3000.013 §6, LLP 3000.031, LLP 3000.036 §17, LLP 3000.040

## 1. Evidence and boundaries

Wave 16's paired bakeoff has frontend medians 200 and 75.5 nonblank
lines. Starter production examples count as frontend even when never imported.
The report attributes all 50 pairs using the unchanged harness buckets.
FRONT owns the additive React surface and completeness presentation here.
RULES5 owns refusing-clause traces and role implications; LANG4 owns other
notes; DAY5 owns starter adaptation; KIT4 owns clock caps and test sessions;
STAGE owns provisioning. SEC owns security invariants. No change to contract.ts,
authorization, completeness certificates or protocol is proposed here.

## 2. A short common read

Wave-20 list presentation addition: LLP 3000.000.000.001 §10 defines the
packaged ReadState component; the query hooks below retain their contracts.

Add `useQueryData(op, args, options?)` in `snapback4/react`, shared by React
and Expo. It delegates directly to `useQuery` and returns `R | undefined`:
loading gives undefined; present, absent and partial give their exact data
(including null, empty lists, false and zero). A denied read throws
`QueryRefusal extends Error`, carrying the original typed `why: Refusal`.
An error boundary may render it; callers choosing inline refusal UI use
`useQueryResult`, which returns `{data: R | undefined, why: Refusal | null,
read: Read<R>}` without throwing. These hooks do not substitute an empty list for loading/refusal. They add
no cache, previous-argument retention or observation ownership. `read` is the same snapshot as `useQuery`.

A data-only read does not promise completeness or freshness. It is for
screens that show the authorized rows that arrived. Pagination, absence proofs,
and actions depending on fresh or complete results continue to use `useQuery`
or `usePage` and their metadata. The precise API remains unchanged. Skipping
uses the existing typed arguments and `{skip: true}`. Required/mistyped
arguments still fail typechecking; result types come from generated operations.
`useMutation(op).run` is already a typed callable and remains unchanged.

Add `<SignedIn fallback={...} restoring={...}>...</SignedIn>` inside
AppProvider (bare SnapbackProvider is insufficient), using the same
`useAuth()` snapshot as AppProvider. Signed-in children mount only in that
state; restoring defaults to null, signed-out uses fallback. The existing
AppProvider account boundary resets account-owned state. Children must be JSX
components (React elements), not eagerly invoked component functions.
No bundled login UI or new session runtime is added to the React entry.

## 3. Completeness notes

Default check output leads with a checked repair against the author's own
query when the binder provides one. Print each distinct repair body once,
including different query names sharing one explanation. Otherwise print the
specific missing pin or the analyzer limitation, never invent a complete form.
The next line states impact: displaying returned rows can keep the query;
proving absence or all matches needs completeness and the precise API.
The final line names any audience proved complete. Verbose keeps full evidence.
Within one invocation deduplicate only identical findings (code, site, message
and repair), preserving different scans at the same operation site. Deduplicate
identical repair bodies separately. Recheck output follows the existing dev
watch lifecycle; no persistent acknowledgement is introduced. No persistent suppression
file, diagnostic acceptance syntax, severity change or new check exit is added.

## 4. UTC hour of day

Keep `hour(t)` as the continuous epoch hour needed by bucket keys and ranges.
Add `hourOfDay(t)` for UTC clock hours 0 through 23. It requires one present
time, returns int, and equals floor(t / 1h).rem_euclid(24), including negative
times. Lower it to existing Calendar and integer arithmetic; no new wire enum
or device runtime requirement. Index/group-key use is outside this addition
(the repeating clock hour is not a chronological key). Existing offset forms
of hour remain unchanged. Guide examples distinguish both at a fixed instant.
Clock cap/session changes belong to KIT4.

## 5. Sweep-only deadline advisory

Add advisory `N_SWEEP_DEADLINE` for a scheduled Quarry job which compares
now (or its periodEnd argument) with a row's time column and directly updates a string/bool column to a literal, when a mutation reads
that table and mentions a column in require/if which either the job updates,
or both the job tests and the mutation directly updates (the §8 coupling),
but has no direct time comparison on the corresponding row deadline. This
is a conservative syntactic warning, not a proof of a vulnerability or safety.
Name job, mutation, table and deadline. Explain sweep lag and ask for an
explicit comparison of that bound row deadline with now, with the intended
opening/closing direction and equality boundary. Do not invent a guard:
opening transitions and expiry transitions have opposite directions. Do not warn on
jobs without a schedule or on mutations already comparing that row deadline.
Limit claims to direct read/loop bindings and update expressions. Upserts,
int-coded statuses, read-filter-only tests, helper-mediated and computed
transitions may be missed. Suppression recognizes direct comparisons on the
same row alias, not semantic row identity; another alias or a helper guard
can cause a false positive. Any such comparison suppresses the advisory,
without proving that it enforces a deadline. FRONT owns this initial lint;
LANG4 owns unrelated notes and can maintain subsequent lint presentation. No runtime behavior or authorization changes. Tests include
positive, guarded, unrelated-clock, unscheduled, opening, equality-boundary
and unrelated-table cases.

## 6. Starters and qualification

Stop emitting recipes/production into ordinary projects. Package the same
recipe sources as build artifacts under node_modules/snapback4/dist/recipes;
the guide copies from that location when explicitly requested by the builder.
This is explicitly assigned to FRONT by work item 1, including permission
to remove starter-shipped recipe files; it changes no DAY5 adaptation behavior
or STAGE provisioning design. Existing projects keep any previously emitted
copies. New copies come from the installed, version-pinned tarball; integration
chooses the next package version without this branch bumping it. The existing
packed production qualification executes the exact guide commands.
Native smoke remains proof support owned by DAY5 and is attributed separately.
Every changed guide snippet runs from the installed pack; additive React hooks
get type inference, refusal, empty/partial data, skip, identity and subscription
lifecycle tests. Three evidence copies are rewritten and checked with the same
line counter without editing original lanes or dropping application features.

## 7. Review and implementation record

2026-10-18: Muse round 1 and fresh Codex rounds 1–2 found incorrect sweep
repair direction and loss of same-site scan/audience evidence. The RFC and
implementation now keep every distinct finding, deduplicate exact repair bodies
and repeated context separately, qualify audiences with the scan, and never
invent the temporal guard direction. Scope/version custody, syntactic lint
limits, AppProvider and identity tests were clarified. Fresh Codex round 3
and Muse round 2 both returned READY on the revised design and implementation.
Verbatim outputs with invocation provenance are under llp/reviews/3000.013.002-*
(the initial Muse stdout includes quota failure and successful key fallback).
This remains Review until implementation qualification is recorded; no author
acceptance or publication is inferred from the technical reviews.

## 8. Wave 16 source-repro correction: confirmation versus overdue

Read-only copies of the actual schemas showed that the first §5 trigger catches
020 releaseVehicle but misses 043 confirmItem. The wedding sweep tests
`not i.confirmed and now >= i.deadline` and sets `overdue: true`; confirmation
checks `not i.confirmed` and writes `confirmed: true`, but never checks overdue.
A status-only gate lint misses exactly the reported omission.

Extend the same advisory, without changing runtime behavior: after finding a
scheduled job's direct deadline comparison and literal string/bool update,
also consider a mutation which both mentions another field of that source
table in require/if and directly updates that field to a string/bool literal,
when the job also mentions it in require/if. This is a syntactic coupling,
not a proof that the job flag or guard belongs to the time comparison's branch.
The output reports the actual syntactic facts about columns, without claiming
that a condition controls or precedes the update or that two aliases are the
same row. It names mutation, job, alias and actual compared time field. The
sweep-written flag may lag; the mutation-written field is not called overdue. The same direct
row-deadline comparison suppresses it; no safety proof or direction is invented.

This catches a confirmation/overdue relationship while leaving unrelated flags
quiet. 046 acknowledgement already computes escalation using a direct deadline
comparison and remains quiet. Table-only conditions can correlate different rows
or branches, so the documented false positives and alias/helper limits remain.
Tests cover the coupled case, a direct deadline guard, an unrelated flag and a separated-branch correlation;
actual 020/043/046 source-copy results are retained in the report. The single
N_SWEEP_DEADLINE code and original exact-time semantics remain unchanged.

## 10. Wave-20 loop repairs (DX5)

Commission: Charlie Cheever's wave-20 fix list #4, #6, #7, #8;
implementer Codex, 2026-10-21. This amendment is proposed until its two
independent reviews and implementation evidence are recorded. SEC5 owns
security intent, campaigns and exposure output; this lane changes lookup
advice, never the authority granted by an application rule.

### Clock reports

Amend LLP 3000.000.000.002 §4's proof helper return: `advance(duration)`
returns `{now, fired, held}`. Each entry carries the schedule name, target,
reason, boundary/window metadata and bounded run summary from the existing
operator-only schedule report. `now()` still returns a number. The CLI and
HTTP advance reply expose the same arrays, retaining `schedules` for existing
operator consumers. Fired means a schedule was enqueued, not that its job
succeeded; the run summary distinguishes parked or pending work. Every
non-fired schedule appears in held with a reason, including an already
processed boundary, a future saved boundary, initialization, a future window
or an explicit hold. Saved boundaries survive restart: one fire per boundary,
coalescing missed periods unless eachBoundary was explicitly requested.
The helper and CLI print saved-boundary holds and their recovery action on
stderr, including the equal-boundary case on a reused store. Advance never
rewinds markers or replays a completed boundary. Reply limits account for the
retained report plus its partition into fired/held. Staging's expiry notice
describes expiry/upload recovery without development-door instructions.
Ambiguous or failed advances are never retried; time may already have moved.
Maintenance failures return an explicitly incomplete schedule report with the
advanced time; the proof helper attaches this reply to its thrown error.

### Source diagnostics without an existence oracle

Extend LLP 3000.036.002 §15.2's N_LOOKUP_FILTERED advice to required reads
and reads of written tables. A later write and an asserted `! else CODE` do
not prove read authority. Existing key/flow proofs still suppress advice,
including prior successful exact reads and deliberately public projections.
One note names the source lookup, authored hiding rule, declaration and the
null/custom-refusal consequence; duplicate N_LOOKUP_HIDDEN notes at that
site are avoided (the more specific N_LOOKUP_HIDDEN takes precedence). This is missing-proof advice, not a claim every caller
is refused. Program `exists` and rule-witness `exists` keep distinct semantics.

For private tables with secondary unique keys, source advice explains that
an unreadable conflicting row uses generic E_RULE (or an undisclosed write
target miss), even when the unique declaration has `else CODE`. The named
constraint applies only when the conflict can be disclosed. Do not return
the declared code only on hidden conflicts: that would violate LLP 3000 §7
and LLP 3000.036.002 §15.2. Public E_RULE repair text, if changed, is identical
for ordinary rule rejection and hidden collision. No row, key, actual hiding
clause or hidden-dependent meter enters the public refusal. Rewrites propose
a minimal readable key projection or operator-mediated ownership/claim flow,
never broader disclosure of private rows. Paired hidden/missing lookup tests assert equality of public diagnostics and
logical meters. Hidden/ordinary write-refusal tests compare public refusals
and receipts; write replies expose no meter.

### Upgrade advice reaches the old CLI

Extend §8's bounded scan with candidate `data ... --json` consumers in authored
scripts, including argv-array and shell forms. List paths without printing
source. State the one-line migration `JSON.parse(stdout).rows` for the former
bare array, and preserve `more`/`limit`. This is a candidate scan, not an AST
proof or an automatic rewrite; report scan limits.

The installed wrapper runs project warnings before the first ordinary check
for that installed version, independently of the runtime-history receipt.
A warning receipt is written only after delivery to a terminal (or an empty
scan). Piped/captured stderr cannot establish delivery: old quiet upgrades
discard it, so a nonempty scan stays unacknowledged and the next ordinary
check repeats the advice, including in CI. Baseline checks neither deliver
nor acknowledge warnings; read/receipt failures do not fail compilation.
Warnings always use stderr, preserving one-object JSON stdout and quiet-mode
visibility. This reaches the usual published 0.4.33/0.4.35 upgrade path because
their upgrade invokes the newly installed wrapper for check. A fresh-process
resolution must select new bytes after package replacement. Direct upgrade
keeps its existing pre-install warning behavior. Test both published old CLIs
against the fresh packed package in an isolated local registry, including
--quiet and --json --quiet and the deferred first check; do not claim
an upgrade was tested by invoking only the new helper.

### Frontend and starter custody

Extend LLP 3000.013.002 §2 with `ReadState` in snapback4/react: a list read
(or PageHook) renders loading, denied, empty, or authorized data through one
component. It adds no subscription, cache or authority. It preserves partial
and stale rows; empty means the returned list is empty, never proven absence.
Loading and empty default to null; denied uses the supplied renderer or
throws the existing QueryRefusal for an error boundary. Children receive the
typed list and precise settled read metadata. Browser and React Native
callers supply their own display elements.

The Expo adapter owns `snapback4-expo/metro` (CommonJS withSnapback(config))
and its wasm transformer. Resolve Expo's transformer relative to the consuming
project/config, compatible with isolated package-manager installs. Preserve
the caller's config and append the existing .snapback4 block and wasm support.
New starter Metro config only obtains Expo's default and calls withSnapback.
The adapter also owns the native smoke sign-in hook; new starters import it
instead of carrying src/native-smoke.ts. Password and guest selection remain
explicit; ordinary launches never sign in automatically. Adapt removes a recognized old helper only when the planned source no longer
references it; custom references, modified helpers and unknown source symlinks
retain application custody. Old generated Metro prose remains recognizable
when adapting an untouched README. No native engine or simulator custody changes.

Amend LLP 3000.011.003.000 §7.2: adapt replaces an untouched todo frontend
proof with a runnable proof scaffold retaining frontend:proof/proof:web and
Playwright. It starts the correct frontend and checks rendered content, then
explicitly refuses to claim an app-specific write proof until the builder
adds one. A public schema without identity or personas skips sign-in and still
checks rendering before the missing-scenario refusal. A custom proof is never
rewritten. Fresh non-todo starters get the
same scaffold. Every Expo starter includes playwright-core, including custom
schemas. Qualification runs changed guide/starter sources from the packed
tarball, tests the expected scaffold refusal, and records frontend character
reductions on copies of three wave-20 Expo lanes. No original lane is edited.

No test.sh, publication, push or version bump is authorized by this work.

review transcripts and source provenance live in llp/reviews/dx5/.

//! Store-neutral authority decisions over typed transaction capabilities.
//!
//! Storage adapters expose identity and work facts. This module alone walks
//! admission families and decides whether a session or claim still stands.

use crate::RuntimeError;

#[cfg(test)]
pub(crate) mod test_cases;

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Authority {
    pub family: String,
    pub generation: i64,
    pub expires_at: i64,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub(crate) struct SessionRecord {
    pub principal: String,
    pub family: String,
    pub kind: String,
    pub digest: String,
    pub scope: SessionScope,
    pub cap_expires_at: Option<i64>,
    pub principal_generation: Option<i64>,
    pub expires_at: i64,
}

impl SessionRecord {
    pub(crate) fn identity_is_valid(&self) -> bool {
        refuse_nul(&[(&self.principal, "this session is not live; sign in again"),
            (&self.kind, "this session is not live; sign in again"),
            (&self.family, "this session is not live; sign in again")]).is_ok()
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub(crate) struct WorkRecord {
    pub principal: String,
    pub state: String,
    pub claimed_by: Option<String>,
    pub session_digest: Option<String>,
    pub lease_until: Option<i64>,
    pub deadline: Option<i64>,
    pub admission: AdmissionRecord,
    pub service_generation: Option<i64>,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub(crate) struct AdmissionRecord {
    pub family: Option<String>,
    pub generation: Option<i64>,
    pub expires_at: Option<i64>,
}

/// The durable facts the authority kernel may read in one store transaction.
/// Implementations perform access only; the policy below interprets records.
pub(crate) trait AuthorityTransaction {
    fn principal_generation(&self, principal: &str) -> Result<i64, RuntimeError>;
    fn session_by_digest(&self, digest: &str) -> Result<Option<SessionRecord>, RuntimeError>;
    fn latest_live_family_member(
        &self,
        principal: &str,
        family: &str,
        now: i64,
    ) -> Result<Option<SessionRecord>, RuntimeError>;
    fn work_by_id(&self, id: &str) -> Result<Option<WorkRecord>, RuntimeError>;
    fn work_by_claimed_session_digest(
        &self,
        digest: &str,
    ) -> Result<Option<WorkRecord>, RuntimeError>;

    fn invalidate_session(&self, digest: &str) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>;
    fn invalidate_family(&self, principal: &str, family: &str) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>;
}

pub(crate) enum AdmissionStanding {
    SessionNotLive,
    AuthorityNotLive,
    Live(Authority),
}

/// Resolve the credential captured at admission. Refresh may replace its row,
/// but the family and principal generation remain the durable authority.
// @ref LLP 3000.009#5.1 — action authority is bound at admission and walked inside one transaction.
pub(crate) fn admission_authority(
    tx: &impl AuthorityTransaction,
    token_digest: &str,
    principal: &str,
    now: i64,
) -> Result<AdmissionStanding, RuntimeError> {
    let Some(session) = tx.session_by_digest(token_digest)? else {
        return Ok(AdmissionStanding::SessionNotLive);
    };
    if !session.identity_is_valid() || session.scope != SessionScope::Full || session.principal != principal || session.expires_at <= now {
        return Ok(AdmissionStanding::SessionNotLive);
    }
    let generation = tx.principal_generation(principal)?;
    let Some(standing) = family_authority_expiry(tx, principal, &session.family, generation, now)?
    else {
        return Ok(AdmissionStanding::AuthorityNotLive);
    };
    Ok(AdmissionStanding::Live(Authority {
        family: session.family,
        generation,
        expires_at: session.expires_at.min(standing),
    }))
}

/// Walk a refresh/delegation family at one transaction snapshot. The admitted
/// expiry is deliberately not an ongoing cap: refresh lifetime and admission
/// lifetime are distinct. Claim leases and deadlines remain hard caps.
pub(crate) fn family_authority_expiry(
    tx: &impl AuthorityTransaction,
    principal: &str,
    family: &str,
    generation: i64,
    now: i64,
) -> Result<Option<i64>, RuntimeError> {
    if refuse_nul(&[(principal, "identity is not live"), (family, "identity is not live")]).is_err() { return Ok(None); }
    let current_generation = tx.principal_generation(principal)?;
    let (mut family, mut generation, mut bound) = (family.to_owned(), generation, i64::MAX);
    let mut seen = std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
    loop {
        if refuse_nul(&[(&family, "identity is not live")]).is_err()
            || generation != current_generation || !seen.insert(family.clone()) {
            return Ok(None);
        }
        let Some(session) = tx.latest_live_family_member(principal, &family, now)? else {
            return Ok(None);
        };
        if !session.identity_is_valid() || session.scope != SessionScope::Full { return Ok(None); }
        bound = bound.min(session.expires_at);
        if session.kind != "action" {
            return Ok(Some(bound));
        }
        let Some(parent) = tx.work_by_claimed_session_digest(&session.digest)? else {
            return Ok(None);
        };
        if parent.principal != principal || !claim_window_is_live(&parent, now) {
            return Ok(None);
        }
        let lease = parent.lease_until.expect("a live claim has a lease");
        bound = bound.min(lease).min(parent.deadline.unwrap_or(i64::MAX));
        if let Some(service_generation) = parent.service_generation {
            return Ok((service_generation == current_generation).then_some(bound));
        }
        match (parent.admission.family, parent.admission.generation) {
            (Some(next), Some(next_generation)) => {
                family = next;
                generation = next_generation;
            }
            (None, None) => return Ok(Some(bound)), // Legacy work and development personas.
            _ => return Ok(None),
        }
    }
}

pub(crate) fn work_authority_expiry(
    tx: &impl AuthorityTransaction,
    work: &WorkRecord,
    now: i64,
) -> Result<Option<i64>, RuntimeError> {
    if refuse_nul(&[(&work.principal, "identity is not live"),
        (work.admission.family.as_deref().unwrap_or(""), "identity is not live")]).is_err() { return Ok(None); }
    if let Some(generation) = work.service_generation {
        return Ok((tx.principal_generation(&work.principal)? == generation).then_some(i64::MAX));
    }
    match (&work.admission.family, work.admission.generation, work.admission.expires_at) {
        (Some(family), Some(generation), Some(_)) => family_authority_expiry(
            tx,
            &work.principal,
            family,
            generation,
            now,
        ),
        _ => Ok(Some(i64::MAX)), // Legacy rows and development personas.
    }
}

fn claim_window_is_live(work: &WorkRecord, now: i64) -> bool {
    work.state == "running"
        && work.lease_until.is_some_and(|lease| lease > now)
        && work.deadline.is_none_or(|deadline| deadline > now)
}

pub(crate) fn claim_is_owned(work: &WorkRecord, worker: &str, digest: &str) -> bool {
    work.claimed_by.as_deref() == Some(worker) && work.session_digest.as_deref() == Some(digest)
}

pub(crate) fn live_claim_authority(
    tx: &impl AuthorityTransaction,
    digest: &str,
    now: i64,
) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
    let Some(work) = tx.work_by_claimed_session_digest(digest)? else {
        return Ok(false);
    };
    Ok(claim_window_is_live(&work, now) && work_authority_expiry(tx, &work, now)?.is_some())
}

pub(crate) fn held_claim_authority(
    tx: &impl AuthorityTransaction,
    id: &str,
    worker: &str,
    digest: &str,
    now: i64,
) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
    let Some(work) = tx.work_by_id(id)? else {
        return Ok(false);
    };
    if !claim_is_owned(&work, worker, digest) {
        return Ok(false);
    }
    live_claim_authority(tx, digest, now)
}

pub(crate) fn live_session(
    tx: &impl AuthorityTransaction,
    digest: &str,
    now: i64,
) -> Result<Option<SessionRecord>, RuntimeError> {
    let Some(session) = tx.session_by_digest(digest)? else {
        return Ok(None);
    };
    if !session.identity_is_valid() || session.expires_at <= now {
        return Ok(None);
    }
    // @ref LLP 3000.033#1.3 — revocation generations survive an older executable.
    if session.scope == SessionScope::Read
        && (session.cap_expires_at.is_none_or(|cap| cap <= now)
            || session.principal_generation.is_none()
            || session.principal_generation.unwrap() < tx.principal_generation(&session.principal)?) {
        return Ok(None);
    }
    if session.kind == "action" && !live_claim_authority(tx, digest, now)? {
        return Ok(None);
    }
    Ok(Some(session))
}

pub(crate) fn live_service_session(
    tx: &impl AuthorityTransaction,
    digest: &str,
    principal: &str,
    now: i64,
) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
    Ok(tx.session_by_digest(digest)?.is_some_and(|session| {
        session.identity_is_valid() && session.scope == SessionScope::Full && session.principal == principal && session.kind == "service" && session.expires_at > now
    }))
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, serde::Serialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "lowercase")]
pub enum SessionScope { Full, Read }

// @ref LLP 3000.009.001.000#9.1 — identity fails closed on both stores, including pre-upgrade SQLite rows.
// Lives here, not in `identity`, because authority is compiled without the SQLite feature.
pub(crate) fn refuse_nul(values: &[(&str, &str)]) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    for (value, message) in values {
        if value.contains('\0') { return Err(RuntimeError::Auth((*message).into())); }
    }
    Ok(())
}

//! A process-local clock for development and explicitly classified staging stores.
use snapback4_core::error::Refusal;
use std::time::{Duration, Instant};

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.001#8-wave-18-developer-loop-amendment-dx4 — preserve expiry tests and tell callers how to recover.
pub(crate) const SESSION_NOTICE: &str = "Time travel also advances session expiry and unplaced-upload collection: seven-day password sessions may now be expired. Sign in again after the jump and upload unplaced bytes after it; never replay an ambiguous clock advance.";

pub(crate) const PRESERVED_SESSION_NOTICE: &str = "Staging sessions keep their remaining lifetime across this jump; sessions:expire explicitly tests credential expiry. Unplaced uploads still age. Never replay an ambiguous advance.";

const MAX_TIME: i64 = snapback4_core::value::I53_MAX;

pub fn input(message: impl Into<String>) -> Refusal { Refusal::new("E_CLOCK_INPUT", "clock", message).guide("clock") }
pub fn release() -> Refusal { Refusal::new("E_CLOCK_DEV_ONLY", "clock", "clock controls require dev or a deploy --staging store; production always uses wall time").guide("clock") }

/// Isolated time tests stay inside an assigned range, or request a free port.
pub(crate) fn isolated_command() -> String {
    isolated_range(std::env::var("PORT_LO").ok().as_deref(), std::env::var("PORT_HI").ok().as_deref())
}
fn isolated_range(lo: Option<&str>, hi: Option<&str>) -> String {
    match (lo.and_then(|n| n.parse::<u16>().ok()), hi.and_then(|n| n.parse::<u16>().ok())) {
        (Some(lo), Some(hi)) if lo > 0 && hi >= lo => format!("dev --memory --port {lo}-{hi} within the current range"),
        _ => "dev --memory --port 0".into(),
    }
}

pub fn duration(text: &str) -> Result<i64, Refusal> {
    if text.starts_with('-') { return Err(input("clock advance cannot move backwards; use a positive duration such as +2m")); }
    let text = text.strip_prefix('+').unwrap_or(text);
    let split = text.bytes().position(|b| !b.is_ascii_digit()).unwrap_or(text.len());
    let (number, unit) = text.split_at(split);
    let multiplier = match unit { "ms" => 1, "s" => 1_000, "m" => 60_000, "h" => 3_600_000, "d" => 86_400_000, "w" => 604_800_000, _ => 0 };
    number.parse::<i64>().ok().and_then(|n| n.checked_mul(multiplier)).filter(|n| *n > 0 && *n <= MAX_TIME)
        .ok_or_else(|| input("expected a positive whole duration with ms, s, m, h, d or w, such as +25h"))
}

pub fn offset(text: &str) -> Result<i64, Refusal> {
    if let Some(rest) = text.strip_prefix('-') { duration(rest).map(|n| -n) } else { duration(text) }
}

fn time(value: Option<i64>) -> Result<i64, Refusal> {
    value.filter(|n| (0..=MAX_TIME).contains(n)).ok_or_else(|| input("time must be Unix milliseconds in 0..9007199254740991; choose a smaller time or duration"))
}

pub fn run_now(text: Option<&String>) -> Result<i64, Refusal> {
    let now = snapback4_core::now_ms();
    match text {
        None => Ok(now),
        Some(text) if text.starts_with('+') => time(now.checked_add(duration(text)?)),
        Some(text) => time(text.parse().ok()),
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub struct Clock {
    dev: bool,
    base: i64,
    offset: i64,
    started: Instant,
    epoch: String,
    revision: u64,
    #[cfg(test)]
    frozen: bool,
}

impl Clock {
    pub fn new(dev: bool, offset: i64) -> Result<Self, Refusal> {
        if !dev && offset != 0 { return Err(release()); }
        Ok(Self { dev, offset, base: time(snapback4_core::now_ms().checked_add(offset))?, started: Instant::now(),
            epoch: snapback4_core::ids::mint(snapback4_core::now_ms(), &mut snapback4_core::ids::Mint::default()).map_err(|e| e.refusal())?, revision: 0, #[cfg(test)] frozen: false })
    }
    #[cfg(test)]
    pub(crate) fn freeze(&mut self, now: i64) { self.base = now; self.frozen = true; }
    pub fn now(&self) -> i64 {
        #[cfg(test)] if self.frozen { return self.base; }
        if !self.dev { return snapback4_core::now_ms(); }
        self.base.saturating_add(self.started.elapsed().as_millis().min(MAX_TIME as u128) as i64).min(MAX_TIME)
    }
    /// Identifies samples taken before/after a restart or deliberate clock jump.
    pub fn offset(&self) -> i64 { self.offset }
    pub fn revision(&self) -> String { format!("{}:{}", self.epoch, self.revision) }
    pub fn presence_now(&self) -> i64 {
        // Production grace is elapsed real time, unaffected by wall-clock corrections.
        if self.dev { self.now() } else { self.started.elapsed().as_millis().min(MAX_TIME as u128) as i64 }
    }
    /// Back to wall time: the offset `--clock-offset` and every advance added
    /// is dropped (a development reset's `{"clock":"reset"}`).
    pub fn reset(&mut self) -> Result<i64, Refusal> {
        if !self.dev { return Err(release()); }
        self.revision += 1;
        self.offset = 0;
        self.base = snapback4_core::now_ms();
        self.started = Instant::now();
        #[cfg(test)] { self.frozen = false; }
        Ok(self.base)
    }
    pub fn advance(&mut self, milliseconds: i64) -> Result<i64, Refusal> {
        if !self.dev { return Err(release()); }
        if milliseconds <= 0 { return Err(input("clock advance cannot move backwards; use a positive duration such as +2m")); }
        let next = time(self.now().checked_add(milliseconds))?;
        let offset = self.offset.checked_add(milliseconds).ok_or_else(|| input("cumulative clock offset overflow"))?;
        self.revision += 1;
        self.offset = offset;
        self.base = next;
        self.started = Instant::now();
        Ok(next)
    }
}

/// The control CLI only talks to a loopback dev host. It does not open its store.
pub fn command(options: &crate::verbs::Options) -> i32 {
    let result = (|| -> Result<String, String> {
        let draining = options.positionals == ["drain"];
        if !draining && (options.positionals.len() != 2 || options.positionals[0] != "advance") {
            return Err(input(format!("usage: {}", crate::cli_flags::synopsis("clock"))).to_string());
        }
        let duration_text = if draining { "0ms" } else { options.positionals[1].as_str() };
        if !draining { duration(duration_text).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?; }
        let release_path = options.flags.get("release").map(|dir| std::path::PathBuf::from(dir).join("store.sqlite"));
        if release_path.is_some() && options.flags.contains_key("port") { return Err("clock --release does not accept --port".into()); }
        let (address, instance, token) = if let Some(path) = &release_path {
            let store = snapback4_core::store_port::AnyStore::open_read_only(path).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
            if store.deployment_environment().map_err(|e|e.to_string())?.as_deref() != Some("staging") { return Err(release().to_string()); }
            let address = crate::store_owner::inspector_address(path).ok_or("staging clock requires a running serve")?;
            let session = crate::serve::token::mint_live(path, "clock", 1).map_err(|(_,error)|error)?;
            (address,None,Some(session.token))
        } else { let (address,instance)=crate::verbs::dev_address(options)?; (address,instance,None) };
        let mut body = serde_json::json!({"duration":duration_text,"eachBoundary":options.flags.contains_key("each-boundary"), "drain":!options.flags.contains_key("no-drain"), "allowHeld":options.flags.contains_key("allow-held")});
        if options.flags.contains_key("expire-sessions") { body["sessions"] = "expire".into(); }
        if draining && options.flags.contains_key("no-drain") { return Err(input("clock drain does not accept --no-drain").to_string()); }
        let body = body.to_string();
        // The common HTTP/1.0 exchange is EOF-delimited and bounds replies at
        // 64 MiB, enough for the preflight's complete explicit window report.
        let response = if let Some(token) = &token {
            crate::verbs::run_forward::exchange_authorized(address,"POST","/dev/clock/advance",None,Some(token),&body,Duration::from_secs(60))
        } else { crate::verbs::run_forward::exchange_dev(address, "/dev/clock/advance", instance.as_deref(), &body, Duration::from_secs(60)) };
        let (status, json) = response.map_err(|error| {
            let evidence = crate::verbs::run_forward::exchange(address, "GET", "/dev/status", None, "", Duration::from_secs(5)).ok()
                .map(|(_, status)| format!("now={}, skewMs={}", status["now"], status["skewMs"]))
                .unwrap_or_else(|| "clock status unavailable".into());
            format!("clock advance reply failed at {address}: {error}; time may already have advanced ({evidence}); inspect /dev/status and queued work before retrying; for an isolated dev pass --port N")
        })?;
        if let Some(notice) = json["sessionNotice"].as_str() { eprintln!("clock: {notice}"); }
        if let Some(held) = json["held"].as_array() {
            for schedule in held.iter().filter(|s| s["holdCode"] == "saved-boundary") {
                eprintln!("clock: schedule {}: {}", schedule["name"].as_str().unwrap_or("?"), schedule["reason"].as_str().unwrap_or("held"));
            }
        }
        if let Some(denied) = json.get("denied") {
            if json.get("now").is_some() {
                eprintln!("clock: incomplete advance report (time already moved): {}", serde_json::json!({"now":json["now"],"fired":json["fired"],"held":json["held"]}));
            }
            let clock = json["message"].as_str().map(|message| format!("; time remains advanced: {message}")).unwrap_or_default();
            return Err(format!("{}: {}{clock}\n  guide: clock", denied["code"].as_str().unwrap_or("E_CLOCK_INPUT"), denied["message"].as_str().unwrap_or("clock advance failed")));
        }
        if status != 200 { return Err(format!("dev refused clock advance: {json}")); }
        Ok(json.to_string())
    })();
    match result { Ok(body) => { println!("{body}"); 0 }, Err(error) => { eprintln!("{error}"); 1 } }
}

 succeeded in 0ms:
//! Staging clock preservation: one shared authority decision on both stores.
use crate::{RuntimeError, authority::{self, AuthorityTransaction, SessionScope}};
use super::{Engine, effects::OutboxTransaction};

pub(crate) const PAGE: usize = 128;

pub(crate) struct ClockSession {
    pub digest: String,
    pub created_at: i64,
}

pub(crate) struct ClockMint {
    pub invocation: String,
    pub intent: String,
    pub principal: String,
    pub family: String,
    pub generation: i64,
    pub expires_at: i64,
}

/// Bounded facts and exact timestamp writes, under the existing writer fence.
/// Worker-marked families are excluded from this enumeration: their timestamps
/// remain owned by the existing worker-lease primitive.
pub(crate) trait SessionClockTransaction: AuthorityTransaction {
    fn clock_sessions(&self, read: bool, after: Option<&str>) -> Result<Vec<ClockSession>, RuntimeError>;
    fn set_clock_session(&self, digest: &str, scope: SessionScope, created_at: i64, expires_at: i64, cap: Option<i64>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>;
    fn clock_mints(&self, after: Option<(&str, &str)>) -> Result<Vec<ClockMint>, RuntimeError>;
    fn set_clock_mint(&self, invocation: &str, intent: &str, expires_at: i64) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>;
}

fn shifted(value: i64, delta: i64) -> Result<i64, RuntimeError> {
    value.checked_add(delta).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Input("staging clock advance overflows a session timestamp; use a smaller advance".into()))
}

fn sessions(tx: &impl SessionClockTransaction, delta: i64, before: i64, apply: bool) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    for read in [false, true] {
        let mut after = None;
        loop {
            let page = tx.clock_sessions(read, after.as_deref())?;
            if page.is_empty() { break; }
            for candidate in &page {
                let Some(session) = authority::live_session(tx, &candidate.digest, before)? else { continue; };
                // Delegated action credentials remain capped by their claim and
                // product deadline. The worker primitive owns their lease shift.
                if session.kind == "action" { continue; }
                let created_at = shifted(candidate.created_at, delta)?;
                let expires_at = shifted(session.expires_at, delta)?;
                let cap = session.cap_expires_at.map(|cap| shifted(cap, delta)).transpose()?;
                if apply { tx.set_clock_session(&candidate.digest, session.scope, created_at, expires_at, cap)?; }
            }
            after = page.last().map(|s| s.digest.clone());
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}

fn mints(tx: &impl SessionClockTransaction, delta: i64, before: i64, apply: bool) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    let mut after: Option<(String, String)> = None;
    loop {
        let page = tx.clock_mints(after.as_ref().map(|(invocation, intent)| (invocation.as_str(), intent.as_str())))?;
        if page.is_empty() { break; }
        for mint in &page {
            if mint.expires_at <= before || authority::family_authority_expiry(tx, &mint.principal, &mint.family, mint.generation, before)?.is_none() { continue; }
            let expires_at = shifted(mint.expires_at, delta)?;
            if apply { tx.set_clock_mint(&mint.invocation, &mint.intent, expires_at)?; }
        }
        after = page.last().map(|m| (m.invocation.clone(), m.intent.clone()));
    }
    Ok(())
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.002#4-staging-clock — staging can preserve live credential lifetime while moving application time.
pub(crate) fn preserve(tx: &impl SessionClockTransaction, delta: i64, before: i64) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    if delta < 0 { return Err(RuntimeError::Input("staging clock advance must be non-negative".into())); }
    // Validate the complete change with bounded pages before the first write.
    // The writer transaction gives both passes the same authority snapshot;
    // shifts never change token keys, scope, family or revocation generations.
    sessions(tx, delta, before, false)?;
    mints(tx, delta, before, false)?;
    mints(tx, delta, before, true)?;
    sessions(tx, delta, before, true)
}

impl Engine {
    /// Staging preserves credential lifetime by default; explicit expiry proofs
    /// pass false. Application deadlines and retry backoffs continue to advance.
    pub fn advance_staging_clock_at(&mut self, milliseconds: i64, before: i64, preserve_sessions: bool) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let tx = self.store.transaction()?;
        if preserve_sessions { preserve(&tx, milliseconds, before)?; }
        tx.shift_worker_leases(milliseconds, before)?;
        tx.commit()?;
        Ok(())
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
pub(crate) mod test_cases;
use super::Tx;
use crate::{RuntimeError, authority::SessionScope, engine::session_clock::{ClockMint, ClockSession, SessionClockTransaction, PAGE}};
use rusqlite::params;

impl SessionClockTransaction for Tx<'_> {
    fn clock_sessions(&self, read: bool, after: Option<&str>) -> Result<Vec<ClockSession>, RuntimeError> {
        if read && !super::identity::present(self.conn())? { return Ok(Vec::new()); }
        let sql = if read {
            "SELECT token_digest,created_at FROM _read_sessions WHERE (?1 IS NULL OR token_digest > ?1) ORDER BY token_digest LIMIT ?2"
        } else {
            "SELECT token_digest,created_at FROM _sessions WHERE (?1 IS NULL OR token_digest > ?1) AND COALESCE(family,token_digest) NOT IN (SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key = 'worker:session-family' UNION SELECT substr(key,15) FROM _meta WHERE key LIKE 'worker:family:%') ORDER BY token_digest LIMIT ?2"
        };
        Ok(self.conn().prepare_cached(sql)?.query_map(params![after, PAGE as i64], |r| Ok(ClockSession { digest: r.get(0)?, created_at: r.get(1)? }))?.collect::<Result<_, _>>()?)
    }
    fn set_clock_session(&self, digest: &str, scope: SessionScope, created_at: i64, expires_at: i64, cap: Option<i64>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        match scope {
            SessionScope::Full => { self.conn().execute("UPDATE _sessions SET created_at = ?2, expires_at = ?3 WHERE token_digest = ?1", params![digest,created_at,expires_at])?; }
            SessionScope::Read => { self.conn().execute("UPDATE _read_sessions SET created_at = ?2, expires_at = ?3, cap_expires_at = ?4 WHERE token_digest = ?1", params![digest,created_at,expires_at,cap])?; }
        }
        Ok(())
    }
    fn clock_mints(&self, after: Option<(&str, &str)>) -> Result<Vec<ClockMint>, RuntimeError> {
        let scope: bool = self.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM pragma_table_info('_session_mint_bindings') WHERE name = 'scope')", [], |r| r.get(0))?;
        let sql = if scope {
            "SELECT invocation,intent,principal,family,principal_generation,expires_at FROM _session_mint_bindings WHERE (?1 IS NULL OR (invocation,intent) > (?1,?2)) AND scope = 'full' ORDER BY invocation,intent LIMIT ?3"
        } else {
            "SELECT invocation,intent,principal,family,principal_generation,expires_at FROM _session_mint_bindings WHERE (?1 IS NULL OR (invocation,intent) > (?1,?2)) ORDER BY invocation,intent LIMIT ?3"
        };
        Ok(self.conn().prepare_cached(sql)?.query_map(params![after.map(|a| a.0),after.map(|a| a.1), PAGE as i64], |r| Ok(ClockMint { invocation: r.get(0)?,intent: r.get(1)?,principal: r.get(2)?,family: r.get(3)?,generation: r.get(4)?,expires_at: r.get(5)? }))?.collect::<Result<_, _>>()?)
    }
    fn set_clock_mint(&self, invocation: &str, intent: &str, expires_at: i64) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.conn().execute("UPDATE _session_mint_bindings SET expires_at = ?3 WHERE invocation = ?1 AND intent = ?2", params![invocation,intent,expires_at])?;
        Ok(())
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::{engine::session_clock::{self, test_cases}, identity::{IdentityTransaction, Session, SessionScope}};
    fn connection(store: &crate::store_port::AnyStore) -> &rusqlite::Connection {
        match store { crate::store_port::AnyStore::Sqlite(store) => store.conn() }
    }
    fn seeded() -> super::super::Store {
        let mut store = super::super::Store::memory().unwrap();
        let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
        let read = Session { token: "ro.install".into(), principal: "install".into(),kind: "member".into(),expires_at: 1,cap_expires_at: Some(1),scope: SessionScope::Read };
        tx.insert_read_session(&read,0,None).unwrap();
        tx.conn().execute_batch("DELETE FROM _read_sessions").unwrap();
        tx.conn().execute_batch(test_cases::SEED).unwrap();
        tx.commit().unwrap();
        store
    }
    #[test]
    fn staging_clock_preserves_only_live_credentials() {
        let mut store = seeded();
        let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
        test_cases::preserves_only_live(&tx);
        tx.commit().unwrap();
    }
    #[test]
    fn staging_clock_overflow_refuses_before_any_write() {
        let mut store = seeded();
        let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
        test_cases::overflow_is_validated_before_writes(&tx);
        tx.rollback().unwrap();
    }
    #[test]
    fn staging_clock_preserves_every_page_and_keeps_read_schema_lazy() {
        let mut store = super::super::Store::memory().unwrap();
        let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
        for n in 0..(PAGE * 3 + 1) {
            tx.conn().execute("INSERT INTO _sessions VALUES (?1,'alice','member',0,200,?1)", [format!("token-{n:05}")]).unwrap();
        }
        session_clock::preserve(&tx,1_000,100).unwrap();
        let count:i64 = tx.conn().query_row("SELECT count(*) FROM _sessions WHERE expires_at=1200 AND created_at=1000",[],|r|r.get(0)).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(count,(PAGE * 3 + 1) as i64);
        assert!(!super::super::identity::present(tx.conn()).unwrap());
        tx.rollback().unwrap();
    }
    #[test]
    fn staging_clock_write_failure_rolls_back_and_expire_mode_is_explicit() {
        let store = seeded();
        let mut engine = crate::Engine::new(store,crate::Schema::default(),[]).unwrap();
        connection(engine.store()).execute_batch("CREATE TRIGGER refuse_read BEFORE UPDATE ON _read_sessions BEGIN SELECT RAISE(ABORT,'injected'); END").unwrap();
        assert!(engine.advance_staging_clock_at(1000,100,true).is_err());
        assert_eq!(connection(engine.store()).query_row("SELECT expires_at FROM _sessions WHERE token_digest='full'",[],|r|r.get::<_,i64>(0)).unwrap(),200);
        connection(engine.store()).execute_batch("DROP TRIGGER refuse_read").unwrap();
        engine.advance_staging_clock_at(1000,100,false).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(connection(engine.store()).query_row("SELECT expires_at FROM _sessions WHERE token_digest='full'",[],|r|r.get::<_,i64>(0)).unwrap(),200);
    }
}
//! Row authority and dormant cell provenance are separate facts.
// @ref LLP 3000.014.000#implementation-proof-clarifications — validate the immediate manual policy before using older live evidence.
use super::*;
use crate::schema::through::{PARENT_DEADLINE, DEADLINE};

pub(super) fn mode(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str)->Result<Option<u8>,RuntimeError> {
    if !live_activation::active(tx)? {return Ok(None);}
    Ok(tx.conn().query_row("SELECT CASE kind WHEN 'source-manual' THEN 0 WHEN 'source-captured' THEN 1 ELSE 2 END FROM temp._live_restored WHERE kind IN ('source-manual','source-captured','source-live') AND t=?1 AND col='' AND id=?2",params![t,id],|r|r.get(0)).optional()?)
}
pub(super) fn record(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str,old:&Table)->Result<(),RuntimeError> {
    if !live_activation::active(tx)? {return Ok(());}
    let kind=match &old.through {None=>"source-manual",Some(e) if e.live.is_some()=>"source-live",Some(_)=>"source-captured"};
    tx.conn().execute("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO temp._live_restored VALUES(?1,?2,'',?3)",params![kind,t,id])?;
    if let Some(edge)=old.through.as_ref().filter(|e|e.live.is_none()) {
        for col in edge.fields.keys().map(String::as_str).chain([PARENT_DEADLINE,DEADLINE]) {
            tx.conn().execute("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO temp._live_restored VALUES('source-capture',?1,?2,?3)",params![t,col,id])?;
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}
fn live_cell(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str,col:&str)->Result<bool,RuntimeError> {
    Ok(tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_values WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND column_name=?3 AND live_source=1)",params![t,id,col],|r|r.get(0))?)
}
/// Captured-only restoration preserves its old audience. A manual row with
/// live-origin inputs must prove its *current* audience before recapture.
pub(super) fn validate(tx:&Tx<'_>,next:&Schema,sources:&BTreeMap<String,Table>,name:&str,old:&Table,row:&Value,now:i64,narrowed:bool)->Result<(),RuntimeError> {
    let Some(table)=next.table(name) else {return Ok(())};
    let t=tx.num(name)?;let id=row.field("id")?.as_str()?;
    if needs_proof(tx,t,id,old,table,narrowed)? {
        crate::engine::through_activation::forward_authority(tx,next,sources,name,old,row,now,true)?;
        tx.conn().execute("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO temp._live_restored VALUES('live-authority',?1,'',?2)",params![t,id])?;
    }
    Ok(())
}
/// Never let unused live cells replace a captured row's own audience/deadline.
/// Missing live-origin inputs remain absent until the capture barrier fills
/// just those new inputs from the current parent.
pub(super) fn overlay(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str,old:Option<&Table>,table:&Table,row:&mut Value,values:Vec<(String,String)>)->Result<(),RuntimeError> {
    let captured=old.and_then(|t|t.through.as_ref()).filter(|e|e.live.is_none());
    for (col,data) in values {
        if !table.columns.contains_key(&col) {continue;}
        let protected=captured.is_some_and(|e|e.fields.contains_key(&col) || matches!(col.as_str(),PARENT_DEADLINE|DEADLINE));
        if protected {continue;}
        let stale=captured.is_some() && live_activation::active(tx)? && live_cell(tx,t,id,&col)?;
        if let Value::Row(fields)|Value::Map(fields)=row {
            if stale {fields.remove(&col);} else {fields.insert(col,serde_json::from_str(&data).map_err(|e|RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?);}
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}
pub(super) fn live_input(table:&Table,column:&str)->bool {
    table.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|e|e.live.is_some() && (e.fields.contains_key(column) || matches!(column,PARENT_DEADLINE|DEADLINE)))
}
/// Current policy inputs are refreshable; the immutable parent lifetime and
/// authored references remain bound even when old policy targets disappear.
pub(super) fn obsolete_ref(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str,column:&str,table:&Table,old:Option<&Table>)->Result<bool,RuntimeError> {
    let Some(edge)=&table.through else {return Ok(false)};
    if column==PARENT_DEADLINE || !(edge.fields.contains_key(column) || column==DEADLINE) {return Ok(false);}
    let protected=if mode(tx,t,id)?.is_some() {
        tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM temp._live_restored WHERE kind='source-capture' AND t=?1 AND id=?2 AND col=?3)",params![t,id,column],|r|r.get::<_,bool>(0))?
    } else {old.and_then(|t|t.through.as_ref()).is_some_and(|e|e.live.is_none() && (e.fields.contains_key(column) || column==DEADLINE))};
    if protected {return Ok(false);}
    Ok(edge.live.is_some() || live_activation::active(tx)? && live_cell(tx,t,id,column)?)
}

/// Reserve declared parent scratch only for callbacks that can fetch it.
/// Captured-only restoration keeps its existing actual-image admission.
pub(super) fn forward_fetch(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str,old:Option<&Table>,table:&Table)->Result<bool,RuntimeError> {
    match old {Some(old)=>needs_proof(tx,t,id,old,table,false),None=>Ok(false)}
}
/// One predicate for actual parent fetches and their preflight reservation.
fn needs_proof(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str,old:&Table,table:&Table,narrowed:bool)->Result<bool,RuntimeError> {
    if !live_activation::active(tx)? || old.through.is_some() {return Ok(false);}
    let Some(edge)=&table.through else {return Ok(false)};
    if edge.live.is_some() {return Ok(true);}
    for col in edge.fields.keys().map(String::as_str).chain([PARENT_DEADLINE,DEADLINE]) {
        if live_cell(tx,t,id,col)? {return Ok(true);}
    }
    if !narrowed {return Ok(false);}
    let history:bool=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_values WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND column_name=?3 AND live_source=0)",params![t,id,PARENT_DEADLINE],|r|r.get(0))?;
    Ok(!history)
}
pub(super) fn live_row(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str,old:&Table,table:&Table)->Result<bool,RuntimeError> {
    if !live_activation::active(tx)? {return Ok(false);}
    if old.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|e|e.live.is_some()) || table.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|e|e.live.is_some()) {return Ok(true);}
    for col in table.columns.keys() {if live_cell(tx,t,id,col)? {return Ok(true);}}
    needs_proof(tx,t,id,old,table,true)
}
