// Device time is an estimate, never an input to server authorization or settlement.
import type { DeviceCore } from "./local.ts";

const WINDOW_MS = 60_000;
const MAX_SAMPLES = 32;
const SHORT_RTT_MS = 1000;
const clocks = new WeakMap<typeof fetch, ServerClock>();
const readers = new WeakMap<() => number, ServerClock>();
const responses = new WeakMap<Response, { clock: ServerClock; order: number }>();
interface Sample { offset: number; roundTrip: number; received: number; provisional: boolean }

/** One bounded, minimum-round-trip window, reset on a server clock revision.
 * A clock advance must not compete with faster samples from before the jump. */
export class ServerClock {
  private offset = 0;
  private issuedOffset: number | undefined;
  private last = 0;
  private samples: Sample[] = [];
  private revision: string | undefined;
  private order = 0;
  private epochBarrier = 0;
  private listeners = new Set<(shift: number, reschedule: boolean) => void>();
  private save: ((value: string) => Promise<void>) | undefined;
  private saving: Promise<void> = Promise.resolve();
  private lastSave: { owner: (value: string) => Promise<void>; value: string; result: Promise<void> } | undefined;
  constructor(private wall: () => number = Date.now) { readers.set(this.issuedDeviceNow, this); }
  fork(): ServerClock {
    const copy = new ServerClock(this.wall);
    copy.offset = this.offset; copy.issuedOffset = this.issuedOffset; copy.last = this.last;
    copy.samples = this.samples.slice(); copy.revision = this.revision;
    copy.order = this.order; copy.epochBarrier = this.epochBarrier;
    return copy;
  }
  /** Unfloored estimated server time: samples and server-answer refetches. */
  estimatedServerNow = (): number => Math.min(Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER, Math.max(0, Math.round(this.wall() + this.offset)));
  private deviceCandidate = (): number => Math.min(Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER, Math.max(0, Math.round(this.wall() + (this.issuedOffset ?? 0))));
  /** Monotonic issued device time: execution, prediction, expiry and rendering. */
  issuedDeviceNow = (): number => this.last = Math.max(this.last, this.deviceCandidate());
  /** Adopt a committed admission without changing the server-time estimate. */
  raiseIssued(time: number): void {
    if (!Number.isSafeInteger(time) || time < 0 || time <= this.last) return;
    const shift = time - Math.max(this.last, this.deviceCandidate());
    this.last = time;
    // Even a one-millisecond floor increase may cross a frozen deadline.
    if (shift > 0) for (const listener of this.listeners) listener(shift, true);
  }
  subscribe = (listener: (shift: number, reschedule: boolean) => void): (() => void) => {
    this.listeners.add(listener);
    return () => { this.listeners.delete(listener); };
  };
  sample(serverNow: number, sent: number, received: number, revision?: string, order = ++this.order, provisional = false) {
    if (!Number.isSafeInteger(serverNow) || serverNow < 0 || !Number.isFinite(sent) || !Number.isFinite(received) || received < sent) return;
    this.order = Math.max(this.order, order);
    const revisionChanged = revision !== undefined && revision !== this.revision;
    if (revisionChanged) {
      // A delayed reply from before an advance cannot restore the old window.
      const split = (value: string) => value.split(":");
      const [epoch, version] = split(revision), [priorEpoch, priorVersion] = split(this.revision ?? "");
      if (epoch === priorEpoch ? Number(version) < Number(priorVersion) : this.revision !== undefined && order <= this.epochBarrier) return;
      this.samples = [];
      this.revision = revision;
      // Every request already issued may still have reached the old process.
      if (epoch !== priorEpoch) this.epochBarrier = this.order;
    }
    const before = this.offset, issuedBefore = this.issuedOffset;
    const roundTrip = received - sent;
    // Long responses use a conservative receipt date, never a midpoint that
    // could latch half a server wait into the monotonic floor.
    provisional ||= roundTrip > SHORT_RTT_MS;
    this.samples = this.samples.filter(s => received >= s.received && received - s.received <= WINDOW_MS).slice(-(MAX_SAMPLES - 1));
    this.samples.push({ offset: serverNow - (provisional ? received : (sent + received) / 2), roundTrip, received, provisional });
    // Every short measured sample outranks provisional receipt stamps.
    const best = this.samples.reduce((best, s) => s.provisional !== best.provisional ? (best.provisional ? s : best) : s.roundTrip <= best.roundTrip ? s : best);
    this.offset = best.offset;
    this.issuedOffset = best.offset;
    if (before !== this.offset || issuedBefore !== this.issuedOffset) {
      void this.persist().catch(() => {}); // Device calls/close await the same durable save.
      // Watchers ignore sub-frame noise. A newly eligible issued offset can
      // move even when a short sample merely confirms the server estimate.
      const estimateShift = this.offset - before, issuedShift = (this.issuedOffset ?? 0) - (issuedBefore ?? 0);
      const shift = Math.abs(issuedShift) > Math.abs(estimateShift) ? issuedShift : estimateShift;
      for (const listener of this.listeners) listener(shift, revisionChanged);
    }
  }
  wrap(fetcher: typeof fetch): typeof fetch {
    if (clocks.get(fetcher) === this) return fetcher;
    const wrapped: typeof fetch = async (input, init) => {
      const sent = this.wall(), order = ++this.order;
      const response = await fetcher(input, init);
      const received = this.wall();
      responses.set(response, { clock: this, order });
      const stamp = response.headers.get("x-snapback-time");
      if (stamp !== null) this.sample(Number(stamp), sent, received, response.headers.get("x-snapback-clock") ?? undefined, order);
      return response;
    };
    clocks.set(wrapped, this);
    return wrapped;
  }
  serverDelay(deadline: number): number { return Math.max(0, deadline - this.estimatedServerNow()); }
  deviceDelay(deadline: number): number {
    return this.issuedDeviceNow() >= deadline ? 0 : Math.max(0, deadline - this.deviceCandidate());
  }
  sampleStream(serverNow: number, revision: string | undefined, order: number) {
    const received = this.wall();
    // The server writes this stamp at the end of its hold, not at request send.
    this.sample(serverNow, received, received, revision, order, true);
  }
  async attach(core: DeviceCore) {
    const kept = await core.call({ op: "meta", key: "server-clock" });
    if (kept.denied) throw new Error(JSON.stringify(kept.denied));
    try {
      const state = JSON.parse(String(kept.ok)) as { offset: number; issuedOffset?: number } | null;
      const issuedOffset = state?.issuedOffset ?? state?.offset;
      if (state && Number.isFinite(state.offset) && Math.abs(state.offset) <= Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER && typeof issuedOffset === "number" && Number.isFinite(issuedOffset) && Math.abs(issuedOffset) <= Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER) {
        if (!this.samples.length) this.offset = state.offset;
        this.issuedOffset ??= issuedOffset;
      }
    } catch { /* Missing or malformed clock metadata starts at offset zero. */ }
    // Only admissions restore a durable floor. Old offset metadata's `last`
    // may be a wall-time now() call and must not override a startup correction.
    // The device commits this floor atomically with admission, and retains it
    // after settlement. Pending entries also recover older stores/journals.
    // Backfill legacy admission times transactionally before replay can retire
    // the queue; offset persistence deliberately cannot retain an issued floor.
    const pending = await core.call({ op: "queued", restore_clock_floor: true });
    if (pending.denied) throw new Error(JSON.stringify(pending.denied));
    const floor = await core.call({ op: "meta", key: "server-clock-floor" });
    if (floor.denied) throw new Error(JSON.stringify(floor.denied));
    const issued = Number(floor.ok);
    if (Number.isSafeInteger(issued) && issued >= 0) this.last = Math.max(this.last, issued);
    for (const entry of (pending.ok ?? []) as { now?: number }[]) {
      if (Number.isSafeInteger(entry.now) && entry.now! >= 0) this.last = Math.max(this.last, entry.now!);
    }
    this.save = async value => {
      const reply = await core.call({ op: "set_meta", key: "server-clock", value });
      if (reply.denied) throw new Error(JSON.stringify(reply.denied));
    };
    await this.persist();
  }
  // @ref LLP 3000.019#3.3 — share unchanged offset persistence, never admission floors.
  persist(): Promise<void> {
    if (!this.save) return Promise.resolve();
    const save = this.save, value = JSON.stringify({ offset: this.offset, issuedOffset: this.issuedOffset ?? 0 });
    if (this.lastSave?.owner === save && this.lastSave.value === value) return this.lastSave.result;
    this.saving = this.saving.catch(() => {}).then(() => save(value));
    const saved = this.lastSave = { owner: save, value, result: this.saving };
    // A failed save remains a failure to its callers, and is retried by the
    // next caller. Attaching another device always supplies a new owner.
    void this.saving.catch(() => { if (this.lastSave === saved) this.lastSave = undefined; });
    return this.saving;
  }
  async detach() { const pending = this.persist(); this.save = undefined; await pending; }
}

export const serverClock = (fetcher: typeof fetch): ServerClock => clocks.get(fetcher) ?? new ServerClock();

/** Streaming final stamps are provisional receipt-time samples, never poll midpoints. */
export function sampleChanges(response: Response, body: { serverNow?: number; clockRevision?: string }) {
  const sample = responses.get(response);
  if (sample && body.serverNow !== undefined) sample.clock.sampleStream(body.serverNow, body.clockRevision, sample.order);
}

export function onClock(now: () => number, listener: () => void): () => void {
  return readers.get(now)?.subscribe(listener) ?? (() => {});
}

export function clockDelay(now: () => number, deadline: number): number {
  return readers.get(now)?.deviceDelay(deadline) ?? Math.max(0, deadline - now());
}

## 10. Wave 13: durable evidence and bounded empty clicks (KIT2)

Implementer: KIT2, 2026-10-16, authorized by fix list #3 and H9.
This amendment replaces conflicting §8 defaults; it changes no wire, language,
authorization, adoption, sync, identifier or expiry semantics.

- `renderedText(page,text,{exact=true,within?})` uses Playwright's text matching,
  excludes input/textarea, and supports substring strings and RegExp rows.
  RN-web split nodes require deliberate container scope or `.first()` when
  they represent the same row. `waitForFresh` permits an omitted container;
  its bounded failure includes locator, last count/text and absent/unchanged.
- Logs default to `<cwd>/logs`: H6 deletes the harness TMPDIR, so printing a
  temporary path would not preserve failed evidence. Exact app/ callers whose
  source discovery includes logs choose an explicit artifact directory outside
  that source root. Fresh private files and direct-child custody are unchanged.
  `cli` detects the token verb and captures stdout in memory; `secret:true`
check/adapt advice for custom proofs; KIT3 owns the other helper contracts.
### Explicit upload claims and helper contracts (KIT3, 2026-10-16)

Implementer: Codex, 2026-10-16. `witnessForm(app,event,async mutate => ...)`
from snapback4/witness-web is called synchronously inside the chosen control's
handler. It accepts a DOM event or React's nativeEvent. The armed witness grants
one fresh per-arm Symbol token only if that exact native event is the active
trusted click or its synchronous submit. The supplied mutate closure carries
that token through awaited uploads. No task timing or ambient async-context
inference confers a claim. Unrelated calls to app/client.mutate cannot borrow
it; sharing the closure explicitly transfers the claim and is the caller's
responsibility. Outside a witness arm, it calls the handler with ordinary
app.mutate and has no proof behavior. The helper is not a security boundary.

The claim suspends the one-second no-write timer while the handler runs, inside
the original 30-second arm bound. Handler return/rejection without a durable
call settles no-write. Every first durable call retains operation/canonical
argument checks. A module-private claim key carries the per-arm Symbol value. Presence of a
foreign/stale token disables the active-event fallback, even during a later
trusted click. Stale tokens after re-arm cannot claim another arm; writes
still run normally and are never cancelled or retried. Claimed receipt deadlines
remain separate. Canonical argument comparison stays exact but mismatch messages
name the mismatch without serializing argument values into retained logs.

`E_WITNESS_NO_WRITE` carries `why.reason`: `not-ready` if the initial app.ready
barrier was not successfully resolved at the click, otherwise `returned-early`.
`E_WITNESS_UNCLAIMED` carries `event` for provenance/re-arm and `arguments` for
operation or canonical argument mismatch. The enum values `not-ready`, `returned-early`, `event`, `arguments` are stable; human
messages may change and are described as message changes in release notes.

`waitForFresh` returns `Promise<string>` containing fresh nonempty rendered
text. `renderedText` records a scoped substring locator for an exact string
request; if that exact locator stays absent but its substring exists, the
bounded timeout adds the RegExp / exact:false rewrite. Ambiguity still refuses
immediately and editable values never prove rendered output.
`startDev` additionally returns `now(): Promise<number>`, which fetches the
current authoritative /dev/status clock on each call with a bounded request.
Reset retains simulated time unless explicitly reset; callers must await now()
and must not cache it across reset or advance. The substring diagnostic never
changes matching or returns a substring as success; it only adds a timeout hint.
P20 missing-dist behavior belongs to REG; starter checking belongs to DAY4.

        if (storeRefusal || closed) return { ok: false, why: storeRefusal ?? ended() };
        if (!credentials) await client.close();
      }
      return revoked;
    },
    async signOut() {
      const draining = delivery?.stop(true);
      // Stop ordinary work synchronously; only delivery's own signals receive
      // the bounded grace period. endCredentials/close must not abort them.
      delivery = undefined;
      const bearer = !readGate || readGate.verified ? headers.authorization?.slice("Bearer ".length) : undefined;
      endCredentials();
      // refreshCurrent owns revocation of any late, unkept successor.
      // Close immediately so outstanding work cannot reuse the credentials.
      const closing = client.close();
      if (bearer) void logout(base, bearer, readGate ? transport : doFetch);
      await Promise.all([closing, draining]);
    },
    close() {
      release?.close();
      readWork.clear();
      cancelReadyNotification(); readyNotifications.clear();
      if (closing) return closing;
      closed = true;
      intentPresentation?.clear();
      void delivery?.stop();
      closeWatchAnswers(watches, storeRefusal ?? ended())();
      pendingEvents.notify(); pendingEvents.clear();
      stopClock();
      closingResponses.begin();
      stopRefresh(ended());
      aborter.abort();
      if (retry) clearTimeout(retry);
      if (refreshTimer) clearTimeout(refreshTimer);
      watches.clear();
      linkListeners.clear();
      viewerListeners.clear();
      for (const entry of pending) {
        // The request already dispatched may have committed. Its send owns
        // settlement; cancellation without a receipt leaves the id pending.
        if (entry === sending) continue;
        const outcome = { state: "pending", id: entry.id } as const;
        entry.resolve(outcome);
        history?.remember(outcome);
      }
      writeListeners.clear();
      closing = Promise.allSettled([flushing, ...requests]).then(() => { pending.length = 0; sessionListeners.clear(); closingResponses.finish(); });
      return closing;
    },
  };
  release = servedRelease({ url: base, fetch: doFetch, headers: () => headers, current: () => !closed && credentials && !storeRefusal });
  Object.assign(client, { release });
  client.onSession(() => release?.invalidate());
  client.onViewer(() => release?.invalidate());
  bindFollowing(client, async (op, args, body, signal) => {
    const revision = credentialRevision, epoch = queryEpoch;
    const blocked = () => closed || !credentials || !!storeRefusal || !!changesRefusal || link !== "online" || !!refreshing || pending.length > 0;
    if (blocked()) return;
    const result = await post(`/following/${op.name}`, { ...body, args }, undefined, undefined, false, signal);
    if (blocked() || revision !== credentialRevision || epoch !== queryEpoch || !result.ok) return;
    // Parsed HTTP refusals, including E_READ_ONLY for a raw read token, have
    // ok:true and flow to the controller's refused state without polling.
    return { ...result.json, __followingCurrent: () => !blocked() && revision === credentialRevision && epoch === queryEpoch };
  }, () => closed || !credentials || !!storeRefusal || link !== "online" || pending.length > 0);
  trackDispatch(client, dispatch);
  readWork.bind(client, watches);
  bindWatchPeek(client, watches, () => storeRefusal ?? (closed ? ended() : !credentials ? auth() : null));
  const start = async () => {
    if (closed) return;
    if (readGate) { scheduleRefresh(); await readGate.verify(); return; }
    if (session && !isReadToken(session.token) && session.kind !== "action" && session.expiresAt - clock.issuedDeviceNow() <= refreshBeforeExpiry) void refreshCurrent();
    else scheduleRefresh();
  };
  if (readGate) registerReadVerification(client, () => readGate.verify());
  trackRotation(client, () => refreshing);
  trackClose(client, closingResponses);
  if (!deferStart(options, start)) start();

    return scope != null && scope === intentScope() && epoch === intentEpoch && revision === credentialRevision ? intentPresentation.sync(pending) : [];
  } });

    fn cancel_effect(&self, id: &str, now: i64) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.conn().execute("UPDATE _outbox SET state = 'failed', cancelled = 1, error = 'the effect was cancelled; external effects are not undone', error_code = 'E_EFFECT_CANCELLED', settled_at = ?2, lease_until = NULL, claimed_by = NULL, lease_digest = NULL, next_at = NULL WHERE id = ?1", params![id, now])?;
        Ok(())
    }

    fn shift_worker_leases(&self, milliseconds: i64, before: i64) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let (bound, end) = crate::engine::effects::lease_shift_bounds(milliseconds);
        let supervised: bool = self.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _meta WHERE key = 'worker:session-family' OR key LIKE 'worker:family:%')", [], |row| row.get(0))?;
        if supervised {
        self.conn().execute("UPDATE _sessions SET expires_at = CASE WHEN (?1 >= 0 AND expires_at > ?2) OR (?1 < 0 AND expires_at < ?2) THEN ?3 ELSE expires_at + ?1 END WHERE expires_at > ?4 AND COALESCE(family, token_digest) IN (SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key = 'worker:session-family' UNION SELECT substr(key, 15) FROM _meta WHERE key LIKE 'worker:family:%')", [milliseconds, bound, end, before])?;
        }
        self.conn().execute("UPDATE _outbox SET lease_until = CASE WHEN (?1 >= 0 AND lease_until > ?2) OR (?1 < 0 AND lease_until < ?2) THEN ?3 ELSE lease_until + ?1 END WHERE state = 'running' AND lease_until IS NOT NULL", [milliseconds, bound, end])?;
        // Advancing the dev clock cannot extend a claim past its admitting family.
        self.conn().execute("UPDATE _requests SET lease_until = CASE WHEN family IS NULL OR principal_generation IS NULL OR admitted_expires_at IS NULL THEN CASE WHEN (?1 >= 0 AND lease_until > ?2) OR (?1 < 0 AND lease_until < ?2) THEN ?3 ELSE lease_until + ?1 END ELSE MIN(CASE WHEN (?1 >= 0 AND lease_until > ?2) OR (?1 < 0 AND lease_until < ?2) THEN ?3 ELSE lease_until + ?1 END, COALESCE((SELECT MAX(s.expires_at) FROM _sessions s WHERE s.principal = _requests.principal AND COALESCE(s.family, s.token_digest) = _requests.family), lease_until)) END WHERE state = 'running' AND lease_until IS NOT NULL", [milliseconds, bound, end])?;
        self.conn().execute("UPDATE _sessions SET expires_at = (SELECT lease_until FROM _requests WHERE session_digest = _sessions.token_digest AND state = 'running') WHERE token_digest IN (SELECT session_digest FROM _requests WHERE state = 'running' AND lease_until IS NOT NULL)", [])?;
        Ok(())
    }

    fn expired_effects(&self, now: i64, limit: u64) -> Result<Vec<EffectRun>, RuntimeError> {
        let limit = limit.min(i64::MAX as u64) as i64;
        Ok(self.conn().prepare(&format!("SELECT {COLUMNS} FROM _outbox WHERE state IN ('queued', 'running') AND (deadline <= ?1 OR (state = 'running' AND lease_until < ?1)) ORDER BY seq, id LIMIT ?2"))?
        .query_map([now, limit], read_run)?.collect::<Result<Vec<_>, _>>()?)
    }

     9	
    10	### Session input and compatibility
    11	
    12	Seeded records preserve the actual signup session's kind alongside its
    13	principal, token and numeric expiresAt. They can be passed directly as Session.
    14	Online createClient accepts a legacy incomplete session literal as token-only
    15	transport credentials: its token must be nonempty; partial principal, kind and
    16	expiry metadata never establishes identity, refresh timing or authority. The
    17	server resolves the bearer; session() is null and there is no refresh timer,
    18	as for the existing token option. Complete shapes remain strictly validated
    19	and privately snapshotted. Local/device session input remains complete-only.
    20	E_SESSION lists every missing/invalid field without values and gives the exact
    21	one-line online repair createClient({ url, token }). An ISO expiry in a complete
    22	shape refuses instead of creating a NaN timer. Restored full sessions retain
    23	the existing restore validation and server expiry semantics.
    24	
    25	### Advances, maintenance and retained boundaries
    26	
    27	ClockAdvance remains an object reporting now/fired/held; valueOf returns now
    28	for arithmetic, ordering and new Date(result). This is JavaScript numeric
    29	coercion, not strict-equality compatibility; typed callers use result.now.
    30	
    31	For classified staging, advance(duration) preserves remaining ordinary session
    32	lifetimes by default. The core advances valid full/read session created/expiry
    33	fields, read caps and recoverable mint receipts by the same checked delta,
    34	in the same transaction as existing worker-lease preservation, before publishing
    35	the new process clock. Expired, capped, revoked or superseded authority cannot
    36	become live. Selection uses the authority kernel and bounded pages; overflow
    37	refuses atomically. Existing action delegated sessions and supervised service
    38	families remain owned by the worker-lease path, without double shifting.
    39	Historical admission timestamps are evidence, not an ongoing cap; application
    40	deadlines/backoffs remain business time. Real elapsed time still consumes
    41	credentials. sessions:'expire' explicitly tests expiry with the existing
    42	business clock. Dev retains its existing expiry behavior. Production rejects
    43	all clock controls and never calls preservation. Restart keeps the persisted
    44	credential timestamps and drops only the existing process-local business offset.
    45	This intentional staging-only simulated jump also shifts a read family's cap;
    46	ordinary production read-cap immutability is unchanged.
    47	
    48	advance(duration, {drain:false}) advances time and pauses background maintenance
    49	before releasing the writer. This covers expiry sweeps, scheduled enqueue,
    50	queued job chunks, asset collection, view recount and delivery maintenance,
    51	so a mutation can exercise its own deadline check. Authorization and logical
    52	row expiry still apply to ordinary queries/mutations. The pause lasts until
    53	host.drain(), another draining advance, or process restart. drain() uses the
    54	same authenticated endpoint with duration:'0ms', drain:true, advancing no time
    55	and resuming normal coalesced maintenance. eachBoundary requires drain:true.
    56	The HTTP report includes maintenancePaused; native CLI spells these controls
    57	clock advance D --no-drain and clock drain. Failed/ambiguous requests are never
    58	automatically replayed. A failed drain leaves its advanced time intact and its
    59	report explicitly incomplete; pending maintenance may continue normally.
    60	
    61	A draining advance whose saved future boundary blocks requested work refuses
    62	E_CLOCK_HELD before clock, session or lease effects. The refusal names the
    63	schedule, saved boundary, next window and recovery duration. Fresh staging
    64	storage and continuing with the original offset are recovery choices; saved
    65	markers never rewind. allowHeld:true (CLI --allow-held) explicitly accepts a
    66	retained boundary. Intentional drain:false pauses and dev --hold-schedules are
    67	explicit holds. not-due/initialization remains a successful reported outcome.
    68	Control authorization/classification is checked again under the actual writer.
    69	
    70	### Upgrade advice and future formats
    71	
    72	Advice is scoped to actual version transitions and each behavior's introduction
    73	version. A fresh init/check is silent. Delivered stderr is acknowledged durably
    74	for non-TTY callers too; baseline checks neither emit nor acknowledge advice.
    75	The new upgrade helper retains warning stderr even under --quiet. Published
    76	older quiet CLIs discard their check child's stderr without signalling this;
    77	that historical capture cannot be distinguished from normal non-TTY delivery.
    78	Advice from that old quiet path may therefore be acknowledged without display;
    79	the upgrade changelog and explicit upgrade scan remain available. No ancestor
    80	process inspection or script mutation is introduced.
    81

    83	uses, explicit persistent startDev calls and data --json consumers. Explicit
    84	nearby refusal assertions do not become dev-door warnings. Candidate paths,
    85	never source or credentials, are printed; incomplete scans remain disclosed.
    86	Future known-family store formats refuse with 'a newer Snapback (FORMAT)'.
    87	Read-only SQL inspection validates the format before exposing rows and exits
    88	nonzero on refusal. Already published old binaries cannot be repaired.

   101	registrations remain valid. No-API registration may capture only durable
   102	non-ephemeral mutation handles; direct witness mutate still needs generated API.
   103	Multiple app lifecycle and cleanup must retain the existing ownership semantics.

export const sessionKey = (url: string, readOnly = false) => storeKey("session", url.replace(/\/$/, "")) + (readOnly ? ".read" : "");

/** The session kept for a server, if it has not expired. */
export async function restoreSession(url: string, store: SessionStore = defaultSessionStore, now: () => number = Date.now, readOnly = false): Promise<Session | null> {
  const key = sessionKey(url, readOnly);
  const raw = await store.get(key);
  if (!raw || logoutEpoch(raw) !== null) return null;
  try {
    const session = JSON.parse(raw) as Session;
    if (isSession(session, now())) return session;
  } catch { /* Invalid JSON follows the same cleanup path as an expired session. */ }
  // A store may condition cleanup on the exact bytes returned by get. Let a
  // changed-snapshot or storage failure propagate instead of deleting twice.
  await store.delete(key);
  return null;
}

includes `user`, `guest` and app-defined kinds minted through
`/auth/session` (`member`, `account`). A narrowed service or worker session
would escape supervisor family fencing, and action sessions are
single-purpose already.

**Two ways to get one:**
1. **At sign-in.** The password and guest doors (`POST /auth/login`,
   `/auth/signup`, `/auth/guest`) accept `"scope": "read"` and mint directly
   into `_read_sessions`, with an ordinary session lifetime and
   `cap_expires_at = expires_at`. The oracle signs Bob in this way.
2. **By narrowing.** `POST /auth/narrow`, authorized by an eligible full
   session, mints a read session for the same principal. The scope is always
   `read`; the request names no scope. It starts a **new family**, and
   `cap_expires_at` is the parent's `expires_at` at mint time.
   - Narrowing is **one writer transaction**: take the writer, sample the
     clock, resolve the parent, check eligibility and generation, capture
     the cap, insert the sibling. This is the pattern `logout_session`
     already uses.
   - A concurrent `logoutAll` either happens first, so narrowing is refused,
     or after, so it revokes the sibling through the generation. The SDK
   exposes this as a standalone helper, `narrowSession(url, session)`, since
   a `Session` is plain serializable data.

**Server refresh never extends the cap.** `cap_expires_at` is written once, at
mint, and never updated. `/auth/refresh` on a read session rotates within
its family, copies the cap and the generation, and sets `expires_at =
min(now + ordinary lifetime, cap_expires_at)`. The one-day floor and
duration restart used for full sessions do not apply.
- Read refresh deletes only rows of its own family. It never runs the
  `_sessions` expired-row sweep that full-session insertion performs.
- Every read credential response (sign-in, narrow, refresh) carries
  `capExpiresAt`. The server enforces the cap; the SDK does not schedule refresh from it.
- Refresh refuses `E_AUTH` only once `now >= cap_expires_at`. While the
  session is live, refresh at the cap rotates the token within the family
  and leaves both timestamps unchanged.

before dispatch: only absent, `"full"` and `"read"` are accepted, including on
refresh/logout bodies. Stored authority still wins over a caller's scope claim. Read polls neither enter nor
leave presence; all query branches honor request-scoped `DeliveryManifest::Skip`.

The online SDK probes the exact credential before dispatch or asset credential
export, uses separate `.read` custody, constructs no write owners, and stops
automatic renewal at the immutable cap. Device admission checks the token prefix
before opening or persisting, including restored sessions and refresh successors.
The client card, receipts card, new read-only card and diagnostic registry describe
the implemented surface. No schema capability or device wire format changed.

Acceptance includes paired fresh stores with actual receipt rows and a history
gap, HTTP barriers and replay, overlapping polls, atomic narrowing races, the
previous published 0.4.13 binary, the generated-query Phase-2 oracle, custody and

table roles:
  principal: principal
  role: enum('admin', 'member')
  unique principal
  read <- .principal = viewer
  insert <- viewer = 'service:operator'
  update <- viewer = 'service:operator'
  online only
mutation provision(principal: principal, role: enum('admin', 'member')):
  require viewer = 'service:operator' else NOT_OPERATOR
  return upsert roles[principal] {principal, role}
query myRole():
  return roles[viewer]
table items:
  owner: principal
  deadline: time
  status: enum('pending', 'expired')
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  update <- .owner = viewer
  online only
mutation add():
  return insert items {owner:viewer, deadline:now + 21d, status:'pending'}
mutation approve(id: id):
  let item = items[id] ! else MISSING
  require item.status = 'pending' else ALREADY_SWEPT
  require now < item.deadline else DEADLINE
  return true
query mine():

codex
One material issue:

- **Session preservation lacks client expiry semantics.** [Proposal lines 31–46](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b22-dx6/llp/reviews/dx6/r1-amendment.md:31) shift server-side session timestamps without defining how existing clients learn the new expiry. A read session expiring at `T+1h`, followed by a preserving `+2h` advance, remains valid server-side until `T+3h`. Its client still holds `T+1h`. Observing the advanced server clock triggers [scheduleRefresh()](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b22-dx6/snapback4/packages/snapback4/src/client.ts:488), which calls `endCredentials()`. Read clients cannot refresh to recover.

  This is a semantic compatibility gap, distinct from unfinished implementation: server preservation conflicts with the retained client expiry contract. Define server-authoritative expiry reconciliation for open and restored clients, or separate credential-time semantics. Require an actual retained read-client test across the original expiry, alongside explicit-expiry and real-elapsed-time controls; raw bearer HTTP tests cannot establish this behavior.

Read-only review; no files changed. This verdict concerns the frozen design, not implementation completion.

**NOT READY**
